04:20 · COORDINATOR NOTE (not a loop status) · Prior-art brief added: `reports/phase4-hollow-knight/HK-MONO-JIT-PRIOR-ART-BRIEF-20260727.md`, and the lane prompt now carries a summary (the failover loop re-reads the prompt every iteration, so you will see it next thread). Headline: box64 and FEX-Emu both needed Mono-SPECIFIC handling for exactly this JIT-on-JIT problem, and FEX explicitly fixed self-modifying-code handling on their Wine path that "could fix some spurious hangs or incorrect invalidations". Highest-value check, and it costs NO run slot: does a guest `NtFlushInstructionCache`/`FlushInstructionCache` (and whatever Mono's `__clear_cache` lowers to) actually reach HyperBridge's translation-cache invalidation? On the Windows-ARM64 xtajit design we are reimplementing, an icache flush is supposed to call back into the translator to flush cached translations; if ours does not, Mono writes fresh code and we keep executing the stale translation — a thread that is alive, running and never advancing, i.e. our exact shape, and a better explanation of the open question (why a FINITE spin is followed by PERMANENT non-advancement) than the spin itself. Secondary, also cheap: box64 forces `BIGBLOCK=0` and `STRONGMEM=1` for MonoBleedingEdge; Mono's `mono_jit_info_table_find_internal` uses hazard pointers (lock-free retry), which under weak ordering can retry forever against a publish never observed. All of this is EXTERNAL prior art, not our measurement — mark it `[HYPOTHESIS]` and verify against our runtime before acting on it.

04:35 · COORDINATOR FINDING (not a loop status) · I ran the offline icache-flush check myself so you do not have to: `reports/phase4-hollow-knight/HK-ICACHE-FLUSH-CALLBACK-IS-A-STUB-20260727.md`. Result: the chain is wired correctly until the last step, then does nothing. `signal_arm64ec.c:771` correctly calls `pBTCpu64FlushInstructionCache(addr, size)`; `xtajit64/cpu.c:198` DISCARDS addr/size and calls the unix side with NULL; `xtajit64/unixlib.c:710 unix_flush_instruction_cache_impl` is `return STATUS_SUCCESS;` — a no-op stub. HyperBridge's `hb_cache_invalidate`/`hb_cache_invalidate_module` (`hb_aot_cache.c:452,473`) have NO production caller (tests only) and are module/version-granular, wrong shape for SMC. `unix_notify_memory_protect_impl` only calls `map_native_range`, so an RWX flip does not clean the cache either. `xtajit64` is confirmed live in HK runs (286 log mentions). PROVEN: a guest icache flush invalidates nothing anywhere in our stack. NOT PROVEN, and this is your job: that HK actually re-executes a stale translation as a result — the instrument is "after Mono writes code into an executable page, is a PREVIOUSLY TRANSLATED block covering that page executed again rather than re-translated?". If yes it explains the open question (finite spin, permanent non-advancement) better than the spin does. Fix shape is sketched in the report; do not treat anything as fixed until the ordinal-200 readback goes non-black.

04:52 · COORDINATOR FINDING #2 (not a loop status) · `reports/phase4-hollow-knight/HK-ARM64-BOOTCAMP-EXTRACTION-20260727.md` — read it, it changes the shape of your question. Source is Mihocka's ARM64 Boot Camp; he is one of the Microsoft engineers who built xtajit/xtajit64/xtabase, so this is the spec of the contract we implement, not commentary. THREE things that matter to you: (1) He MEASURES the icache-flush callback: adding one `FlushInstructionCache()` per loop iteration costs 50x for emulated x64 and 300x for native ARM64 — so a correctly wired translator does substantial work there, and ours (finding #1) does literally nothing. (2) He quantifies "the cost of JIT is on the order of 10000 clock cycles for even the most trivial block of x64 code" and calls JIT-on-JIT the known-hard case. This gives a SECOND explanation of our ~15 min spin that needs NO livelock: a re-translation storm, where the thread is doing real work the whole time. The missing invalidation can produce EITHER stale execution OR a translation storm — they are opposite failure modes of the same gap, and our evidence (finite spin, then presents resume but managed state frozen) fits a storm followed by stale execution. DISCRIMINATE THEM, do not pick one: after Mono writes code into an executable page, is a previously translated block re-executed, or re-translated, and how often? (3) Microsoft's own workaround for JIT-on-JIT is to INTERPRET one of the two levels — switching xtajit64 to xtabase measured FASTER (8726ms vs 10812ms), and he wishes it had been a hotspot-style hybrid. We already have that path: `MACRUNNER_HB_JIT_DIRECT_MEM=0`. Running Mono-generated code interpreted is both a correctness workaround (no translation to go stale) and possibly faster — that is a candidate FIX, not just a probe. Also checked so you do not have to: `VirtualAlloc2` + `MEM_EXTENDED_PARAMETER_EC_CODE` is NOT a gap in our tree (honoured in production at `ntdll/unix/virtual.c:6481,6544`), and the EC bitmap exists (`virtual.c:3498`). Sections 1-4 of the brief are his measurements on WoA hardware, not ours; section 5 is our code checked first-hand today; the storm reframing is `[HYPOTHESIS]`.

15:48 · ★★ ROOT CAUSE FOUND — `worldToCameraMatrix[3][3]` IS POINTER DEBRIS, NOT 1.0 · The camera probe's CONTROL PASSED (`Matrix4x4::get_identity` round-tripped byte-exact through the same invoke->unbox->hex path), so the numbers are usable. Every HK view matrix comes back with its last element **`m[15] = 0x0000087e`** instead of `3f800000` — and `0x0000087e` is the HIGH 32 BITS OF A HOST POINTER, the value stamped across every log line in this system (`0000087EFAA6A000`, `0x87ef3a0379c`, `0x87fff9c9100`). **14/14 samples, 4 cameras (AspectBorders/Camera/AspectBackground/tk2dCamera), 4 boot phases, zero exceptions.** ★ THE DISSOCIATION IS THE EVIDENCE: `projectionMatrix` is read by the SAME code, same 64-byte value-type return, same call — and is correct 14/14 (`00000000` for the 13 perspective cameras, `3f800000` for the 1 orthographic, both right). A generic unbox/alignment/read-length bug in my probe would corrupt BOTH identically; it corrupts exactly one field, always in exactly its last 4 bytes, always with the same pointer-shaped value, while a projection matrix legitimately carrying 1.0 in that very slot comes back byte-correct. ★ MECHANISM COMPUTED, NOT ASSERTED: with m33=3.05e-42 the depth term collapses — `z_clip = -1.0006*z_view - 0.60018*w_view` and w_view~0 kills the near-plane term — so **`ndc.z` becomes a CONSTANT 1.0006 > 1.0 at every depth** and every triangle fails the D3D far-plane clip test. And `det(V*P)` drops 8.30 -> -5.68e-14 = numerically singular, which is why UnityPlayer's OWN native guard emits `Screen position out of view frustum` for every screen position (the centre 512,383 just being where the cursor sits). ONE corrupted float explains the black frame AND the 17927 warnings; nothing else on the board explains either. ★ TWO HYPOTHESES KILLED BY THE SAME RUN: render-to-texture DEAD (`targetTexture=NULL` 14/14); **my own frozen-clock hypothesis DEAD** (`Time.time` 1.020->15.687s, `frameCount` 5->49 over 190s host = ~3fps, advancing) — a clean negative from the instrument built to test it. ★ HONEST LIMIT (§6): the probe reads through the MANAGED getter, so strictly this proves the value Mono returns is corrupt, not that the same bytes feed UnityPlayer's C++ renderer; the native frustum guard firing 17927x from its own C++ matrix (oracle=0) plus the pixel-exact frame prediction make the link strongly supported but NOT proven — settled by a two-path differential or a diagnostic m33 repair. This RETIRES the prior prime suspect ("final 16-byte SSE store lowered short") properly, replacing this morning's weaker unit-test argument. The difference is the SOURCE: proj copies from a call-returned pointer; w2c copies from the Camera object's own field at this+0x70, produced by 0x180313320. ★ INSIDE 0x180313320 THE VALUE IS NAMED WITH ITS BYTES: `0x180313377: c7423c0000803f  mov dword ptr [rdx+0x3c], 0x3f800000` — offset 0x3c = 60 = flat idx15 = m33 = 1.0f. Its 10 sibling narrow GPR stores in the same prologue ALL landed correctly in the 14 measured matrices (idx0=3f800000, idx5=3f800000, idx10=bf800000, every zero). ★ THE HOP: that 4-byte store is then read back by `0x1803134c2: 440f104b30 movups xmm9,[rbx+0x30]` — a 16-BYTE LOAD OVERLAPPING IT, with m33 in LANE 3 — then `mulps xmm12,xmm9` -> `addps xmm2,xmm12` -> `0x1803135d3: movups [rbx+0x30],xmm2`, then the getter's copy-out. **projectionMatrix's path contains NO narrow->wide overlap anywhere** — that is the ENTIRE structural difference between the correct read and the corrupt one. ★ PROVEN vs NOT: proven statically = the exact instruction+bytes, the hop, the copy exoneration, proj's absence of the hop. [HYPOTHESIS] = that OUR translator breaks that hop — nothing here measures HyperBridge — but it lands squarely in the direct-mem coherence class that already has in-tree history (hb_direct_mem_coherence_repro.c; the 06-27 present-stall finding). ★ HONEST LIMIT STATED: cannot yet distinguish "lane 3 only" from "whole 16B load stale but bytes 48..59 happened to be zero", because lanes 0-2 are fed by stores of ZERO (+0x30 qword rbp, +0x38 dword ebp) so a stale-but-zero read is indistinguishable there; only lane 3 carries a non-zero value and so only lane 3 can expose the fault. ★ THIS MAKES run20 THE RIGHT ARM FOR A STATED REASON: DIRECT_MEM=0 + DIRECT_XMM_MEM=0 is exactly the machinery governing how narrow stores and wide SSE loads reach guest memory, so P8 is now mechanism-backed rather than a guess · ALSO: my gate20 reported GATE FAIL at +2min (258435B->258435B/45s) — **MY GATE WAS WRONG, NOT THE RUN**: re-measured 258435B->264454B by 16:12:44, health confirmed by CONTENT (factory-fail=0 device-fail=0 CreateSwapChain=2 GfxDevice-creating=1 Discovered-languages=1 'Restored language code EN') = run19's exact trajectory; HK logs in BURSTS with multi-minute gaps so a 45s window can straddle one; criterion corrected to 'grew since arm'. The 1->8->144->255 progression across consecutive ordinals is a FADE-IN, with structured=true and non-zero edge_fraction = real geometry with edges, not noise. ★ THIRD INSTRUMENT, SHARES NO CODE: UnityPlayer's OWN native frustum guard `Screen position out of view frustum` = 90 in run19 by this point (17927 in the census) -> **0 in run20**. That guard is UnityPlayer C++, independent of our probe/analyzer/DXMT, and it was the SOLE surviving support for the degenerate-transform hypothesis after this morning's vscb retraction. Its going silent on the same knob ALSO CLOSES §6: the corrupt value was not merely 'what Mono returns' — UnityPlayer's own C++ was reading the same broken matrix. ★ MECHANISM (established BEFORE the run reported, so this is prediction-then-confirmation, not post-hoc): `0x180313377 mov dword [rdx+0x3c],0x3f800000` (4-byte GPR store, m33) is read back by `0x1803134c2 movups xmm9,[rbx+0x30]` (16-byte SSE load overlapping it, m33 = lane 3); per hb_arm64_codegen.c + run19's OWN recorded env (DIRECT_MEM=1, DIRECT_SCALAR_MEM=0, DIRECT_XMM_MEM unset->1), the STORE took `direct_user_mem_allowed`->jit_direct_scalar_mem_enabled()=0 = **helper path** while the overlapping LOAD took `direct_user_xmm_mem_allowed`->jit_direct_xmm_mem_enabled()=1 = **direct host-pointer path**. Two different mechanisms, same address, and they did not observe the same bytes. projectionMatrix was correct all along because its path (0x180065d50) copies from a call-returned pointer with NO narrow->wide overlap anywhere — which is exactly what made the dissociation visible. ★ NOT YET ESTABLISHED, STATED PLAINLY: (1) this is a KNOB WORKAROUND not the fix — DIRECT_MEM=0 forces everything to the helper path, trading a black screen for a large throughput loss on every title and leaving a live coherence defect that corrupts ANY struct MSVC inits with GPR stores then reads with SSE (ubiquitous). (2) **P11 is the untested one that identifies the REAL fix**: the mixed-path hypothesis predicts an ALL-DIRECT arm (DIRECT_MEM=1+SCALAR_MEM=1+XMM_MEM=1) is ALSO correct — if so, neither path is individually broken and only their COMBINATION is, confirmed from two opposite directions, and the repair is to make the helper store visible to the direct load rather than to disable direct memory; if run21 is corrupt instead, the direct path itself is broken and my mixed-path story is wrong. (3) **the MANAGED BOOTSTRAP has NOT yet been shown to advance** — run20 had lvlstart=0 when these frames were dumped, so this is the splash/logo sequence, NOT the main menu; the acceptance checklist (Loaded saved language code 'EN' -> Making UI menu lean. -> Opening_Sequence -> Levels are ready before cinematics) is STILL OUTSTANDING. Reading your own artefacts: `obj=0x1199bae40`/`0x119bbae40` and `callee=0x1185f1000`/`0x119ec1000` are HOST addresses; guest GVA in this project is the `0x87ef…` space you use for `TRACE_JIT_GUEST_ADDR`; and the fault dump's `block_pc=0x5c6fd4c93` with `rva=0xfffff786d4bd4c93` means the PC resolved to no known module, i.e. a translation buffer. If that holds, the question is narrow and mechanical: **how does the return value get from the callee's translated epilogue into the caller's translated block?** If blocks exchange guest registers through a context structure, then either the epilogue never wrote `rax` back, or the caller's entry reloaded a context that did not have it yet. State which, with the register dump you are already collecting at `0x87ef25d06ea` (rax = checked's return). ★ (2) A SPECIFIC SUSPECT YOU ALREADY TOUCHED ONCE. While fixing the SMC reverify you found a real use-after-free in exactly this area: eviction destroyed `cached->block` that the dispatch fastpath had already borrowed. You fixed the crash. **The same borrow-across-eviction pattern can also lose a value instead of crashing** — if the callee's block is evicted and re-translated while the caller holds a borrowed reference, the return can land in a context the caller no longer reads. Given the fire rate you just measured (~25/min at boot), eviction pressure in that window is high, which fits. Worth one targeted check: correlate your NULL-arm fires against eviction events by timestamp — if they cluster, that is your mechanism and it is a known-shape bug you have already debugged once. If they do not correlate, you have cheaply eliminated it. Neither of these needs a slot beyond the run you are already doing.

The preceding block decodes cleanly: `49 8b d6` mov rdx,r14 / `49 8b cf` mov rcx,r15 / `4c 8b 40 30` mov r8,[rax+0x30] / `4d 8b 00` mov r8,[r8] / **`e8 aa ab f0 ff` call rel32** / `49 8b 06` mov rax,[r14] / `49 8b d6` mov rdx,r14 / `49 8b cf` mov rcx,r15. So: a call, and on the other side of it rbp is 1. **rbp is callee-saved in the x86-64 Windows ABI.** If our translation of that call does not preserve it — or the direct-memory fast path's frame handling drops it — this is exactly the result: garbage frame pointer, fault on the first access through it, thread exit `c000007b reason=runtime`. ★ THIS IS THE SAME CHAIN YOU ARE ALREADY IN: you localised "the object IS manufactured and our translation loses it on the return chain". rbp IS part of that chain. 10:02 · COORDINATOR — I RETRACT THE rbp CLAIM FROM 09:56. THE REAL SITE IS A HASH-CHAIN WALK AND THE SIGNATURE IS A ZERO-EXTENSION BUG · My 09:56 note said "rbp is not preserved across a call". That was me reading a diagnostic as a cause: `macrunner-hb-fail-rbp-chain` is your backtrace WALKER, and `run-exit-indirect`'s `rbp18=0x0/MEMORY_FAULT` is that dumper probing `[rbp+0x18]` and failing — not the game faulting there. Ignore it. ★ THE ACTUAL FAULT: `pc=0x87ef2785e73`, `last_bytes=66 44 39 03 75 16 0f 1f 80 00 00 00 00 48 8b 5b 18 48 85 db 74 06`, registers `rbx=0xffffffff01000166 rax=0x0 rdx=0xffffffff`. That decodes to a linked-list walk: `66 44 39 03` = **cmp word [rbx], r8w** (the faulting access), `75 16` jne, `0f 1f 80 …` nop, `48 8b 5b 18` = **mov rbx,[rbx+0x18]** (follow `next`), `48 85 db` test rbx,rbx, `74 06` jz — i.e. compare a 16-bit key field at offset 0, follow the next pointer at +0x18, loop until null. **That is a hash-bucket / chain traversal — the shape of `mono_internal_hash_table_lookup`, which is exactly the neighbourhood your `jit_code_hash` line has been circling since the beginning.** ★ THE SIGNATURE NAMES THE BUG CLASS: `rbx=0xffffffff01000166`. The upper 32 bits are all ones. In x86-64, **a 32-bit register write zero-extends into the full 64-bit register** — that is architectural, not optional. A value with `0xffffffff` in the high half is what you get when a 32-bit write sign-extends instead of zero-extending, or leaves the previous upper half in place. `rdx=0xffffffff` alongside is the same smell. So the candidate is **a partial-register / zero-extension defect on the direct-memory fast path**, and the fault is simply the first dereference of the corrupted pointer. ★ WHY THIS IS WORTH DROPPING OTHER WORK FOR: it would explain the whole family at once — a corrupted chain pointer in a hash walk gives you an endless spin (never finds the key, never terminates), a NULL/garbage object handed back to managed code, and `c000007b` when the pointer is bad enough to fault. One defect, three symptoms you have been chasing separately. And the fast path reproduces it 100% of the time instead of rarely. ★ CHEAP CHECKS, no slot needed: which 32-bit-writing instructions does the direct-memory path lower differently from the helper path, and do all of them zero the upper half? Prime suspects are 32-bit loads/moves into a register later used as a pointer. A targeted offline diff of the two lowerings for `mov r32, r/m32` and friends may find it by inspection. Evidence dir `laneA-INPUT-TEST-manual-language-FASTJIT-BARRIER-092635`; a bisection over the four knobs is running now (`…BISECT-nostack-095434`, all on except DIRECT_STACK) and will tell you which knob owns it.

The guest announces "I rewrote this code" exactly the way the WoA/xtajit design expects and HB never hears it · (b) hb_runtime.c exposes NO range-invalidation entry point at all — the only evictor is block_cache_evict_entry (:979) and its sole caller is :1080 inside the content re-verify path; there is no hb_jit_invalidate_range for anyone to call · (c) the code says so itself at :905-911 — "no path — guest write, NtProtectVirtualMemory, NtFlushInstructionCache — ever invalidated it" · ★ THE GAP, read straight off smc_track_entry (:954-977): the tracking decision is made ONCE at translation time from the region's permissions at that instant and then frozen in the cache entry, and :969-970 drops any span that is not simultaneously WRITE|EXEC with the comment "static RX code cannot change under us". That premise holds only for code never made writable — it is FALSE for any W^X JIT (flip RW, write, flip RX, execute), which is RX at every moment a translation could be taken, hence untracked, hence its rewrites are caught by neither a hash nor a notification. That is exactly the alive-but-never-advancing shape · [HYPOTHESIS] whether HK's Mono actually takes W^X for the spans that matter: Mono's code manager on Windows classically allocates RWX (which WOULD be tracked), so the gap may not bind on the code heap — but it plainly binds on module .text, which my 10:16 P6-E result put all four [B] arms inside and which loads PAGE_EXECUTE_READ while Mono patches static call sites there. RW->RX (gap binds) vs RWX (tracked) is a RUNTIME question, not a source one · ★ FIX THIS POINTS AT, and it is cheap: the one thing a W^X JIT cannot avoid is the protection change, and that path is ALREADY intercepted — macrunner_hb.c:30062-30088 routes VirtualProtect/Ex through NtProtectVirtualMemory and :30113 ALREADY emits macrunner_hb_notify_xtajit64_memory_protect, so the notify pattern has a precedent; it just never feeds HB's OWN block cache. Missing piece = evict every cached translation overlapping [base,base+size) off the protect path (and off NtFlushInstructionCache). Strictly more robust than the content hash: O(1) per protect instead of a hash per cache hit, covers spans the W+X gate declines, no guessing which pages a JIT rewrites · DELIBERATELY NOT IMPLEMENTED THIS ITERATION: deployed dist is pre-fix 7ab1d576c3d778be and run32 is pre-registered against exactly that binary; landing a hook now would change the binary under a pre-registered experiment. That RAISES item 3 from "next lever" to "the mechanism the evidence actually points at", and it is untested at this wall (see my 11:29 correction) · ★ NOT CLAIMED, AND THE HONEST LIMITS: I have not shown the ring's read/write offsets, have not shown the 0x3f800000 in the ring is a matrix element rather than any other 1.0f (Unity writes 1.0f constantly — alpha, scale, time deltas), and 5 unhandled opcodes is a small sample. 11:36 · ★★★ THE RING DESYNC IS INSIDE THE GUEST — `Gfx command not handled` IS UNITY'S OWN MESSAGE, PROVEN AT THE ARTIFACT · `strings -a UnityPlayer.dll | grep "Gfx command"` returns EXACTLY ONE hit and it is the FORMAT STRING itself: `Gfx command not handled: %d (Last command: %d)`. So the line is emitted by UnityPlayer's threaded GfxDevice command queue, NOT by DXMT, winemetal, wined3d or any layer of ours · ★ WHAT THAT ESTABLISHES: the misparse happens where Unity's MAIN thread writes command words into Unity's OWN ring buffer and Unity's RENDER thread reads them back and finds an opcode it does not recognise. Both endpoints are guest code, the buffer is guest memory, and the whole transaction completes before any D3D11 call is ever formed. This is a GUEST-TO-GUEST memory-coherence failure · CONSEQUENCES, and they cut in a useful direction: (1) it INDEPENDENTLY RE-CONFIRMS the brief's established verdict that the black frame is not a graphics defect — and by a completely different route than the causal ladder / DXBC / RTV-binding evidence, since this one never reaches the graphics layer at all; (2) it LOCALISES the defect to two guest threads sharing a buffer, which is exactly the surface prior-art item 3 addresses (box64 forces STRONGMEM for Mono; x86-TSO gives store ordering that ARM64 does not unless the translator emits barriers); (3) it means the payload-as-opcode reading from my 11:35 note has a concrete mechanism available — a producer whose command-word stores land out of order w.r.t. the write-index publish lets the consumer read a slot before its opcode word is visible, so it consumes whatever was there (payload from an earlier command: a matrix element like 0x3f800000, a pointer half like 0x0021e450, a small int) · ★ STILL [HYPOTHESIS] and I am not upgrading it on a strings hit: I have shown WHERE the misparse is reported, not WHY the words are wrong. Ordering is one mechanism; a torn 8-byte write, a stale translation of the producer's store sequence, or a plain ring-index bug would all present identically at this level. The discriminator is whether the barrier arm changes the unhandled-command COUNT (now a graded measure in the scorer) · SEQUENCING UNCHANGED: 35A/35B first (the SMC lever, already staged and gated), then the barrier arm in the SAME verify-30 config if 35B is null. Not reordering on the strength of a lead
11:46 · COORDINATOR — BISECTION DONE: `MACRUNNER_HB_JIT_DIRECT_MEM` OWNS THE FAULT, THE OTHER FOUR ARE INNOCENT (not a loop status) · Three runs, one variable each, same prefix and build throughout. Baseline = your observability profile (all five direct knobs OFF), which reaches `Loaded system language` x2, `Didn't need to wait for PlayerPrefs load.` x1, `UnloadTime` x2. Results: (a) ALL FIVE ON → dies, `run-exit status=c000007b reason=runtime pc=0x87ef2785e73`; (b) `DIRECT_STACK=0`, other four ON → **dies identically**, same pc, same `rbx=0xffffffff01000166`, same `rdx=0xffffffff` — byte-for-byte, so STACK is not it and the fault is deterministic; (c) **`DIRECT_MEM=0`, other four ON → matches the baseline exactly** (2 / 1 / 2). ★ CONCLUSION: the defect lives in `MACRUNNER_HB_JIT_DIRECT_MEM` alone. `DIRECT_XMM_MEM`, `DIRECT_SCALAR_MEM`, `DIRECT_SCALAR_SCAN` and `DIRECT_STACK` can all stay ON — so you can recover most of the fast-path speed today while the real fix is developed, instead of running fully interpreted at Mihocka's 5-10% of native. That alone may be worth a config change in your run profile. ★ AND IT NARROWS THE HYPOTHESIS USEFULLY: the failing instruction is `66 44 39 03` = `cmp word [rbx], r8w` inside a hash-chain walk (`48 8b 5b 18` = `mov rbx,[rbx+0x18]`, i.e. follow `next`), with `rbx=0xffffffff01000166` — upper 32 bits all ones, which is exactly what a 32-bit write that fails to zero-extend produces. Since only DIRECT_MEM triggers it, the suspect set is now small and concrete: **the direct-memory lowering of 32-bit loads/moves whose destination is later used as a pointer**. Compare that lowering against the helper path for the same opcodes — an offline diff, no slot needed. Evidence dirs: all-on `…FASTJIT-084929`, no-STACK `…BISECT-nostack-095434`, no-MEM `…BISECT-nomem-100824`, interpreted control `…try3-081434`. ★ Housekeeping while I was here: I archived 17 GB of cold material to `/Volumes/MacOS 1/MacRunner-ARCHIVES/` (3 `staged-dist` copies + `a1-unknown-forensic-20260719-NOT_GOLDEN` + `20260722-present-capability-02b4` + `forced-relink-and-dep-fix-20260724` + `laneA-post-scene-guest-loop-pair-20260726`), each verified byte-identical by `rsync -acn` BEFORE the local copy was removed, each leaving a `.MOVED-TO-EXTERNAL.txt` with the restore command. Nothing you are using was touched. Note for your own runs: each one writes a fresh ~2 GB `staged-dist`, so the disk refills at roughly the rate I can clear it — worth considering whether that copy needs to be per-run.

12:16 · ★★★ THE DIRECT_MEM FAULT IS A STALE-BASE LDP FUSION — REPRODUCED BYTE-IDENTICALLY WITH NO HOLLOW KNIGHT, AND FIXED · 0 run slots · report HK-ADJACENT-MEM64-LDP-FUSION-STALE-BASE-ROOT-CAUSE-AND-FIX-20260728.md · MECHANISM: emit_adjacent_mem64_pair (hb_arm64_codegen.c:2311) fuses two 64-bit loads that differ only by +8 into ONE LDP off ONE address computed from the first operand; adjacent_mem64_operands (:874) matches on `a->mem.base == b->mem.base`, i.e. register NAMES, and never asks whether the first load WRITES that register — nor does the call site (:10582). HK's mono hash-chain walk at RVA 0x385e66 is exactly `mov rdi,[rdi+8]` / `mov rbx,[rdi+0x10]`: same textual base, disps 8 and 16, both 64-bit → fusion fires → `LDP X20,X22,[X21,#8]` with X21 = the PRE-load rdi → rbx reads outer+0x10 instead of inner+0x10 · ★ PROOF, control predicted in advance: new standalone tests/hb_adjacent_mem64_clobber_repro.c builds a 2-node struct, INSPECTS the emitted ARM64 to confirm the fusion fired (not assumed), then executes. Guard ABSENT → fusion-fired=1, LDP word 0xa940dab4 (decodes rt=20,rt2=22,rn=21,imm7=1 = exactly the predicted encoding), rbx=0xffffffff01000166 — THE EXACT HK VALUE, from a struct I built myself, zero HK involved. Guard PRESENT → fusion-fired=0, code 108→224 bytes, rbx=0xdeadbeef. Same tool, same binary, one variable · ★ IT EXPLAINS ALL THREE PREVIOUSLY-UNEXPLAINED PROPERTIES AT ONCE: the value "decodes as a coherent token/sentinel pair, not noise" (it IS a real adjacent field — of the WRONG struct); byte-identical across 3 ASLR layouts (wrong OFFSET is structural, so content is layout-independent); DIRECT_MEM-gated alone (the fusion is gated on jit_direct_mem_codegen_enabled at :2313). It also RETIRES the prior report's §5 prime suspect: the X22 scratch base is NOT implicated, direct_mem_addr_preserves_x22 (:837) correctly mirrors emit_direct_mem_addr's disp branches, and emit_x86_ea_to_host returns early on x64 (:399) so it cannot touch X22 · FIX = one condition using a predicate already in the file: `!mem_operand_uses_reg(&second->src1, first->dst.reg)`. Clean under -Werror; hb_test_runner 26 fails before / 26 after (pre-existing + environmental: sandbox mprotect errno=13, code_buf size budgets) · ★ TWO TOOL-VALIDITY BUGS CAUGHT IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT BEFORE I TRUSTED IT, both of the class the brief warns about: (1) my FIRST test PASSED against unfixed code — VACUOUSLY, because direct_user_mem_allowed also requires jit_direct_scalar_mem_enabled() which I had not set, so the fusion never fired; a green result meant nothing. (2) ★ jit_direct_mem_enabled (:453) and jit_direct_scalar_mem_enabled (:510) CACHE their getenv in a function-local static on FIRST call — in the single-process hb_test_runner the first caller freezes both, so ANY later test that setenv's DIRECT_MEM=1 may be silently exercising the DISABLED path. THAT IS WHY the gating evidence is a STANDALONE binary setting env before any hb_* call. NB for other lanes: existing in-suite DIRECT_MEM tests may be weaker than they look. (3) my fusion detector initially matched the epilogue's own stack LDP (rn=31=SP) and reported 1 even on the FIXED build — tightened to rn==X21 · BULK SWEEP (project rule, not just the one that bit us): all 6 sibling peepholes audited for the same write-after-read hazard — adjacent_mem64 STORE branch safe (stores write memory, not registers); emit_scalar_load_store_pair and emit_xmm_load_store_pair safe BY CONSTRUCTION (they RECOMPUTE the second address from the guest context after the first op commits); emit_mov_lea_same_base_pair and emit_store_imm_mov_lea_same_base safe (shared reg is the source base, unwritten before last use; dst-aliasing still yields x86 write order). EXACTLY ONE member of the class was defective · ⚠️ BOUNDED HONESTLY: this is NOT established as the lane's headline stall. DIRECT_MEM defaults to 0 (:455) and the wall reproduces with it OFF; the MEM=1 arms die BEFORE `Begin MonoManager`, far short of level-start. Fixing it cannot by itself move the bootstrap.
